## Block Tower mirrored reach (2026-10-13)

[PR #259](https://github.com/rjdunlap/neo/pull/259), branch `codex/block-tower-mirrored-reach`, claim `1fa37ca`, source base `4ead39a`. Existing level 6 deals one left edge and one right edge in seeded order: two blocks reaching four eighths, then three reaching five. Blocks remain bottom-first in canonical outward coordinates; table, drops, help, bot, star and cartoon fall project to the active side. Strict support, early success, top-only revision and one hint per task remain. The hint and drop ghost follow resize. How-to, inventory and browser case updated; no new levels, bands, saves or couch changes.

The archived slice-5 build packet follows; its future-tense verification text describes the original definition, not current proof. Current checks are in VERIFICATION.md.

5. **Ready, defined; unbuilt: mirror reach content at existing 6** (definition 2026-10-13, `codex/block-tower-mirror-definition`, [PR #254](https://github.com/rjdunlap/neo/pull/254)). Fun action: build out toward a star on either side of a table, watch the support result and revise the top block. Learning purpose: the same equal-block support rule works in either direction; the star's distance and a block's color do not change its weight. This docs-only definition preserves current accounting and needs none of the open decisions above. Dispatch `block-tower-mirror-definition-20261013T110552Z`; worker `01a134f1-36e0-7d63-9e78-3c390d8cb02d`.

   **Tasks and selection.** Stay at saved level 6, school window 5–6, with exactly two tasks in their original order: `(blocks: 2, star: 4)`, then `(blocks: 3, star: 5)`. `star` stays a positive distance in eighths of the original width (`W = 8`), measured beyond the active table edge. Shuffle the two directions `[right, left]` once with the supplied seeded RNG; attach them to those tasks and freeze them for the run. Thus the two possible runs are right/left and left/right; each task has a mirror variant across seeds, without four tasks or a harder distance. Resize, retries, hints and top-block revision neither redraw a direction nor advance a task. Keep equal widths/masses, original block colors, sticker seed drawings and one shell reward after two successes. Reaching with fewer than the available blocks is still accepted; this is not a minimum-block challenge.

   **Table and coordinate model.** Let `d = +1` mean a right edge with the table extending left, and `d = -1` mean a left edge with the table extending right. For screen edge `E` and block width `BW`, physical block middle `x = (screenX - E) * W / BW`; canonical outward middle `u = d * x`. Store/search the canonical middles bottom first, and project them with `screenX = E + d * u * BW / W`. A table is a half-plane in the rule model; its drawing must cover the inward drop domain without introducing a second unsupported edge. Keep the floor-supported (`edge = null`) rule used by level 5 unchanged.

   | Rule / geometry | Right (`d = +1`) | Left (`d = -1`) | Shared canonical rule |
   | --- | --- | --- | --- |
   | Table supports whole-stack mean `m` | `m < 0` | `m > 0` | Mean of `u` is strictly below 0; equality falls |
   | Star line from `E`, in eighths | `+4`, then `+5` | `-4`, then `-5` | Positive `star` stays 4, then 5 |
   | Outward face of a block at `x` reaches | `x + 4 >= star` | `x - 4 <= -star` | `u + 4 >= star`; line contact counts |
   | Accepted physical middle slots | Integers `-12..8` | Integers `-8..12` | The same 21 integer slots `-12..8` |

   For every block above the bottom, the mean of that block and all blocks above it must remain strictly within 4 eighths of the block below's middle. Reflection preserves that absolute-distance check and the lowest falling block index; never reverse the stack's bottom-to-top order. Test stability before reach, so a falling block touching the line cannot complete a task. For example, canonical `[-2, 0]` reaches 4 stably; `[-2, 0, 1]` reaches 5 with every placement stable. Their physical left-edge mirrors are `[2, 0]` and `[2, 0, -1]`.

   **Drops, solver and help.** Normalize pointer movement into `u` before the existing round-and-clamp operation, then project its result back to the active side. This also reflects rounding at half slots; independently rounding signed screen offsets would break symmetry. Reflect the existing forgiving horizontal acceptance region in canonical coordinates, retaining its vertical cutoff. Preview, committed drop, `finish`, `nextReach`, hint ghost and finger bot all use this one domain/projection. Search canonical slots in the existing descending order, requiring each prefix to stand immediately; use no final-state/scaffold search. A physical prefix `xs` at the right edge and its negation at the left edge must have the same failure index, reach result and completion existence, with each returned move reflected. Preserve `finish` results: `[]` for an already stable reach, `null` for an unstable or unfinishable prefix, otherwise a stable continuation. Help uses its first move; when no continuation exists, it highlights the actual top block to remove. Removing that block must return to a prefix with a completion, or allow another top removal until one exists. No hint may offer an unreachable slot.

   **Interaction, accounting and presentation.** Retain immediate settlement after each committed drop, free tray movement and top-only lifting; lock input through settlement/result, return cancelled or out-of-region drops to the tray, clear stale help on pickup and preserve resize/leave cleanup. A topple adds one miss; a full stable stack short of the line adds one miss; after two such failures in a task, constructive help counts once for that task. Further help does not recount it. Do not turn level 6 into unscored experiments or change adaptation, tall-glow timing on 3–4, or unscored level-5 predictions. Mirror the table, star line, placed blocks, drop/help ghosts, support explanation and fall direction together; lay out the tray and edge from `view`, keeping the pet and controls clear and all drag targets at least 100 logical units. The spoken instruction and level-6 how-to must work on either side (for example, “out past this edge”), with the same two-task finish. Device speech and whether a child understands the mirrored edge remain person checks.

   **Implementation exit evidence (not run for this definition).** Use the new-interaction tier: typecheck and unit tests proving both side orders/unchanged targets, exact mirror support and strict edge cases, reach-line equality, reflected half-slot rounding/clamping, and solver/hint/bot symmetry for every offered prefix up to each task's block budget. Include stable completions at every placement, impossible-prefix top-removal recovery and early success. One filtered level-6 `shortlist SHORTLIST_ONLY=tower FROM_LEVEL=6 TO_LEVEL=6` play should cover both side orders, one topple and one full-short miss, one counted hint, top recovery, cancellation/settling/resize, and exactly one saved result/sticker. Run `fingerdemo FINGER_ONLY=block-tower FINGER_LEVELS=6` for both orders, and review one portrait screenshot with the left edge and pet clearance; build only if shared files change. Do not add world/couch/offline runs for this slice. This definition changes only this dive, its tracker, verification and queue; no source, scripts, assets, tests, build or browser work, levels/bands, storage, temporary ropes, couch support or developer decisions are included.

## Peekaboo Barn cover support (2026-10-14)

[PR #294](https://github.com/rjdunlap/neo/pull/294), claim/base `67eeef9` plus working diff on `codex/peekaboo-cover-support`: ready slice 1 pins the original seven sticker/icon animals independently of the play pool; bounds the how-to to levels 1–8 and states eight/twelve free reveals; resolves overlapping taps to the nearest cover, with four covers scaled to 0.82 and starting at x = 150 for pet clearance. ## Penguin Slide: The Frozen Ponds couch course (2026-10-15)

PR #419, `claude/penguin-frozen-course`: a third Penguin Slide course for the couch shelf. `parseBoard` (`src/games/penguin-slide/course.ts`) reads a `B` legend for ice blocks (boards without blocks parse as before). **The Frozen Ponds**, version 1: five block ponds with fewest slides 4, 5, 6, 7 and 9 (minimum 31), fish 1, 1, 2, 2, 2 and blocks 1, 1, 1, 1, 2. Each pond has exactly one shortest route and every block is needed (frozen like a rock, the route is longer or impossible). Unlike Pond Practice, a careless push can trap the penguin, so undo is the way back. `COURSE_IDS` order is practice, ponds, frozen, clouds, ...; the course's rule says a push is a slide and it instructs with the existing `slide.ice` line. Course tests are block-aware (route counting, dead ends, the solver walked through `move()`), with new tests for the legend, block necessity and blocks only in this course. `BROWSER_SUITE=couchcourse` plays it by controller with the solver (Player 1, perfect 31, one sticker, both badges; screenshot `couch-course-frozen`).

Three pictures in a jumble go into the cars of a train, fewest claps at the back to most by the engine on the right; equal counts go either way round. Rules `fitsCar`, `nextForTrain` and `trainCounts` in `logic.ts`; rounds are three trains of three pictures with at least two distinct counts, counts 1-4, no picture twice. A wrong car is a miss (count said and clapped); the second and later misses add a hint that glows the picture for the first empty car; empty-space and loaded-car drops are free. The ghost finger drags the next fitting picture. The how-to card gained the level-6 lines and its level 4 and 5 steps were bounded so they stop leaking onto later levels. [Proof](VERIFICATION-2026-10-16.md#2026-10-16--clap-the-syllables-level-6-beat-train); device speech for the new lines and a child's play are not checked.

## The Woodland Picnic, the second Island Errand (2026-10-16)

[PR #479](https://github.com/rjdunlap/neo/pull/479), `claude/woodland-picnic`, built from its [definition](../definitions/woodland-picnic.md) (chunk 22, W1). For the school band, from Wonder Woods: Hazel the squirrel postkeeper asks three jobs in any order, each a round of a game she knows at the story's level without moving the game's own level: invitations (Mail Carrier 6), baskets (Seesaw Balance 7) and the beat (Beat Builder 3, on its picnic stage). Each finished round changes the clearing (flags on three houses and lit stepping stones; two baskets on a carrying pole; a drum and bell on a stump). With all three done the friends (bear, bunny, duck) walk in along the stones, a stated eight-step picnic beat (`PICNIC_BEAT`, drum and pentatonic bells) plays twice, and Hazel's photo is kept once (`store.endStory('woodland')`). Once kept, a photo button opens it large with a green "tell it again" (`retellStory`; the keepsake stays). Home goes to Wonder Woods. Entry is the first stop on the Wonder Woods path (`StorySpot`) and a "Woodland Picnic" card on the subject view, for band `school` only.

Contract: `WOODLAND_STEPS`, `STORY_STEPS`, `StoryId` and `StoryStep` in `src/content/world.ts`; `stories.woodland` in the save with a default and repair in `migrate()` (an old save gets an empty telling); the store's `completeStep`, `endStory` and `retellStory` take a story ID (default `'picnic'`); `StoryRound` in `App.ts` carries an optional story; route `go.woodland(from?)`. New: `WoodlandScene.ts`, `src/art/woodland.ts`, `src/content/woodland.ts` (and tests), `woodland.*` voice lines. The Windy Picnic is unchanged. Checks are in [VERIFICATION.md](VERIFICATION-2026-10-16.md#2026-10-16--woodland-picnic).

## Dot Link level 11, Find my buddy (2026-10-17)

[PR #524](https://github.com/rjdunlap/neo/pull/524), `claude/dot-link-buddy`, slice 2 of the [dive](../deep-dives/dot-link.md#build-slices-in-order). A toddler entry on a 3×3 two-color board: one adjacent same-color pair, chosen by seeded `pickBuddy` among all such pairs, wiggles from the first second. Drawing between them (either direction, or a longer line through both) pops only that pair, and a new pair lights after the refill. A full two-color 3×3 always has at least two same-color neighbor pairs, so the dive's "exactly one pair" is built as exactly one pair lit; the goal is 4 buddies (the dive gave none; level 2 uses 6 links). Another same-color line shakes, does not pop, boings, says `dots.buddyhint` and counts one miss; a cross-color drag does nothing. The pulse is not a hint, so there is no idle or two-miss hint and hints stay 0. Toddler band `{ min: 1, max: 11, order: [1, 11, 2] }` with `entryLevels: [11]`; the board scales up to 1.4× for a catch area of about 137 units per dot. New voice lines `dots.buddy` and `dots.buddyhint`; the how-to gains a level 11 step and the square step is now bounded to level 5 (open-ended `from: 5` would have claimed levels 6 and 11). Browser: `BATCH_ONLY=dots` now runs levels 1–6 and 11, with `DOTS_LEVELS=11`. Checks in [VERIFICATION](VERIFICATION-2026-10-17.md#2026-10-17--dot-link-level-11-find-my-buddy); open: a toddler's drag on a device.

## Little Conductor levels 1–2, music M2 (2026-10-20)

Branch `claude/little-conductor-m2`, slice M2 of the [study](../deep-dives/subjects/music-instruments.md#build-slices-in-order), from the [definition](../definitions/little-conductor.md). New game `little-conductor` in Music Mountain (the land now holds 6): toddler 1–2, preschool 1–2, pre-K 2–2 (capped at 2 until M3). Three seeded friends (not the bear or pip) on a bandstand play one seeded four-beat pentatonic motif (hop, slide or wave) on the game's own score clock (`advance` in `logic.ts`); the turtle pad is 60 bpm, the rabbit 90. The first pad tap starts the band at once; later changes wait for the next beat, with a pending ring and clock icon, and a tap on the playing pad calls off a waiting change. Four beat marks sit under the active pad; level 2 shows a check beside each pad heard whole. Done is dim until the heard gate (level 1: a whole phrase at the pace last chosen; level 2: both paces heard whole and the chosen one whole since chosen; a waiting change closes the gate at once). An early Done blinks the unlit marks and speaks hear-whole or hear-other, never counted. Listen again restarts the tune without resetting heard marks. At the gate Done plays the tune once more with claps, the band bows, and the round finishes with 0 misses and 0 hints and one sticker. Six idle seconds before the first tap pulse the turtle pad once (never a hint); the band plays at half gain while the pet speaks. `music: silenced(STYLES.jelly)`, added to the registry test's music-making list; new `sfx.pluck(step, gain)` and an optional gain on `sfx.drum`. The ghost-finger bot taps turtle, rabbit once turtle is heard whole, then Done (both levels). Nothing is saved; no couch version. Checks are in [VERIFICATION](VERIFICATION-2026-10-20.md#2026-10-20--little-conductor-levels-12-music-m2).

## Jar Sort levels 1–8 (2026-10-20)

[PR #651](https://github.com/rjdunlap/neo/pull/651), `claude/jar-sort-build`, built from its [definition](../definitions/jar-sort.md). New game `jar-sort` in Rainbow Meadow (8 games), region puzzle-peaks, music `STYLES.paint`, skills planning/sorting/spatial-reasoning; preschool 1–3, pre-K 2–5, school 4–8. Tap a jar to lift its top fruit and another to drop it (same fruit on top, or an empty jar, with room; one piece per move); a drag from jar to jar does the same; the ground or the lifted jar sets it back. Frozen solver-checked boards in `puzzles.ts` (level 1 the two authored boards; 14 per level for 2–8; level 6 defeats the greedy solver). A refused move is free at levels 1–2 (lit target jars) and one miss from 3; every second miss on a board glows the hint move (one hint); a wander hint after `max(8, 2 × par)` legal moves; the hint search is capped at 200,000 states with an undo fallback; a stuck board makes the pet say so and pulses undo, never a miss or hint. Level 8 lids only on a fruit's own mat. Free unlimited undo. A ghost finger taps along the solver's path and the demonstration plays one board. No couch version. Deviations from the definition: the jars slide off screen at the end (the land's host does not carry them); the portrait row arithmetic assumed a 768-wide view, but the upright logical view is 1024 wide, so seven jars use two rows as in landscape; the card's level-3-on line reads "a fruit dropped where it cannot go counts as a mistake" (the card test forbids the word "level"). Checks are in [VERIFICATION](VERIFICATION-2026-10-20.md#2026-10-20--jar-sort-levels-18).

Idea row moved here from the notebook's candidates:

| **Jar Sort** (Water Sort and Ball Sort puzzles; [Ito et al., FUN 2022](https://arxiv.org/abs/2202.09495)) | Move the top fruit between jars until each jar holds one kind. Spatial planning in stacking order; preschool with two kinds to school with five. A free undo is the safety net | **Defined, ready (now built):** [definition](../definitions/jar-sort.md). One piece per move, frozen solver-checked boards, a bounded hint search; Fill and Dump keeps its continuous pour and *Two jugs* | S–M |

## Egg Catch, roll when ready at levels 4–5 (2026-10-20)

Levels 4 (route) and 5 (sort) no longer roll the egg on a clock (it was 3.5 s for the first egg, 2 s after). The egg waits at the top (`held`) until she taps the green arrow, the same top-right button level 7 uses; gates can still be flipped while it rolls, a wrong-way egg is still a miss (`egg.wrongway`), and after two misses the needed gates glow as before and the arrow now also pulses once the gates are set (levels 4, 5 and 7). `routeTouch(gates, exit)` in `logic.ts` gives the ghost finger its moves: flip the needed gates, then tap the arrow; a rule test checks at most two flips and then 'go', with the egg reaching the exit. Voice: `egg.route` and `egg.sort` end with "then tap the green arrow". How-to: steps 4 and 5 name the arrow, with a new note for levels 4–5. The `eggs` case in `scripts/browser-check.mjs` honours `TO_LEVEL`, waits for the held egg, checks it is still waiting after 4.5 s, sets the gates and taps the arrow. Checks are in [VERIFICATION](VERIFICATION-2026-10-20.md#2026-10-20--egg-catch-roll-when-ready-at-levels-45).

## Egg Catch, a miss that teaches and hens that wait at levels 2–3 (2026-10-21)

[PR #720](https://github.com/rjdunlap/neo/pull/720), `claude/egg-catch-miss-teaches`, from the [deep dive](../deep-dives/egg-catch.md) slice 2. At levels 2–3 a missed brown egg (one someone was playing over) gets `sfx.boing()` and the new line `egg.peep` "Peep! Slide under the next one."; a white egg caught in the basket boings too. After two misses (the existing hint, which still slows the fall 1.4×) the hay under the next brown egg's lane glows (a yellow ellipse and ring above the hay piles, `eventMode` none); `glowLane()` in `logic.ts` picks the brown egg in the air that lands first, else the next brown egg in the seeded stream.

- **Hens that wait.** `HenPace` in `logic.ts`: hens lay only while someone plays (touch-down, a steering move, a finger held still, or couch stick/button input through `control()`). An egg that lands with nobody playing since it was laid settles the hens (sleepy look), is not a miss and gets no line; the next touch wakes them after 0.6 s. At level 3 a second egg in the air also waits for play since the last egg, so a round left alone lays one egg and stops. Known consequence: a child who stays perfectly still under the right lane also settles the hens after that caught egg and the next touch resumes; this follows the dive's literal rule ("no touch since it was laid").
- **Bots.** `autotouch` touches once per new egg where the basket already is (and to wake settled hens); the couch `autoplay` gives a small stick nudge for the same; `HenPace.owesPlay` exposes it.
- **How-to.** Level 2–3 note: "After two missed eggs, the hay under the next brown egg glows and the eggs fall slower. The hens rest when nobody is playing and lay again at the next touch."
- **Tests.** An idle round lays one egg with no miss at levels 2 and 3; busy play keeps laying with no unplayed landings; a mid-round look-away costs at most one (level 2) or two (level 3) unplayed eggs and the next touch wakes the hens; the second egg waits for play; the glow lane equals the next brown egg over 100 seeds at both levels.

## Block Garden levels 1–8 (2026-10-20)

[PR #655](https://github.com/rjdunlap/neo/pull/655), `claude/block-garden`, built from its [definition](../definitions/block-garden.md). New game `block-garden` in Rainbow Meadow (now 9 games), region puzzle-peaks, music `STYLES.paint`; pre-K 1–3, school 2–6, grown-up 5–8. Drag fixed seed-tray shapes onto a bed; full rows and columns bloom and empty until every bud blooms (level 8 empties the bed). Rules, solver and generators are in `src/games/block-garden/logic.ts`; frozen gardens (14 per level, 5–8) are in `puzzles.ts`, written by `scripts/block-garden-gardens.mjs`. Touch only, with an `autotouch` drag bot, voice lines `block-garden.*` and `game.block-garden`, a how-to card, and the `blockgarden` browser case. Built on every default in the definition's open decisions. Deviations from the definition:

- **Generation.** Levels 1–4 are built at runtime from per-level templates (one straight gap; a crossing row and column each one domino short; a corner-shaped crossing gap with an optional third row that can only bloom after the column empties; two rows with straight gaps plus a decoy), each checked by the solver and the level filter, 50 tries and then an authored fallback (never needed over 500 seeds in a test). Levels 5–8 are frozen from block templates (a block of rows full of stones but for the planned pieces), solver- and filter-checked. A round shows its garden under one of the square's eight symmetries rather than a left–right mirror; par is kept (tested).
- **Search cap.** It counts states generated (each placement tried), `SEARCH_CAP` 30,000, not 200,000 visited states: an IDA* search with a squares-needed lower bound and a tray-aware placement bound. The worst level-8 off-plan search measured about 320 ms on Node (the definition's target was under 150 ms; an iPad check is open). While she follows the stored solution the hint reads it without searching; past the cap it steps back to her latest state on the stored solution. After each planting a 4,000-state search checks for a dead end.
- **How-to note.** The level-3+ line reads "A shape dropped where it cannot fit counts as a mistake." (a test forbids level talk).

## Domino Run levels 1–8 (2026-10-20)

[PR #659](https://github.com/rjdunlap/neo/pull/659), `claude/domino-run`, built from its [definition](../definitions/domino-run.md) with every default taken. New game `domino-run` in Bubble Beach (6 games), region tinker-lab, music `STYLES.paint`; toddler 1–2, preschool 2–4, pre-K 3–5, school 4–8. Stand dominoes from a tray into a lane's sockets and tap the green starter; a domino falls if the one before reaches it (reach = size) and it is at most one size bigger; a bell is size 1 and a gong size 4 (only a giant). Levels: 1 Tip the line, 2 One missing, 3 Fill the gaps, 4 Tall ones reach farther, 5 Two bells, 6 Growing dominoes, 7 Just enough, 8 Two bells, one tray. A stall is never a miss; the second stall on a line (levels 3–8) glows the next placement and counts one hint, as does the pause sheet's "Show a hint". Extra voice lines beyond the definition: `domino-run.instruct-gap` (level 2) and `domino-run.too-heavy-giant` (level 6). No couch, journal, creation or save field. Rules in `src/games/domino-run/logic.ts`; ghost-finger bot drags the hint placement then taps the starter.

Open note: on the last boo-boo the patient says "...Thank you." and then "All better! Thank you, doctor!", two thank-yous back to back. ## Fruit Slice levels 1–8 (2026-10-20)

Fruit hangs at the top of a slow arc and a swipe splits it: tap at 1, swipe at 2, only one kind at 3, only fruit at 4, stop at the number and send the bowl at 5, two kinds at 6, one straight line through exactly N at 7, pieces by twos at 8. No clock; missed fruit drifts back. Rules in `logic.ts` (closed-form arcs, swept hit test, one miss per stroke); `fruitslice` browser suite; the ghost-finger bot waits until its 180-unit stroke is clear of other fruit. Differences from the definition: the board box is 820×400 units, translated and never scaled; level 7 boards always have lines through N and N−1, and N+1 only below N = 4; level 8's last sign is a still board of three pairs (six fruit); strokes end on pointerup, pointerupoutside or pointercancel (no 0.5 s no-events rule); kind words use per-kind lines. The fractions levels stay a proposal.

## Shape Sorter slice 1, support truth (2026-10-20)

[PR #669](https://github.com/rjdunlap/neo/pull/669), `claude/shape-sorter-support-truth`, [deep dive](../deep-dives/shape-sorter.md) build slice 1 (gaps 2, 4, 6 and 8). Level 1 (plan flag `everyTouch`): a tap on the box gulps and giggles, a tap on the floor or the wall wiggles the nearest waiting piece and names it, and a piece let go far from the hole plops (`sfx.pop`) while the box leans toward it; no misses or hints. Help rule (`wrongDrop`/`startHelp` in `logic.ts`): every second miss since the last glow lights the right hole (one hint each), whichever pieces were tried, and a piece going in does not reset the count. Judgement against the dive's "two wrongs in a row anywhere": streaks that a success resets could pass six misses without a glow at 6–8 pieces, which the dive's own test property forbids, so the count runs since the last glow (this also matches "a glow after two misses"). The three-second speech throttle stays; the boing and shake always happen. Level 8: a shape dropped in its own hole that is not the one asked for is named by `shape.find.notyet.{circle,square,triangle,star,heart,hexagon}` ("That's the {shape}! We're looking for the shape with …"), said every time; it is still one miss and bounces (decision 3 default). `CLASSIC` pins levels 7–8 to the six classic kinds instead of `SHAPES`; `boxTopFor`, `BOX_FRONT` and `FLOOR_AT` in `logic.ts` with a test that every plan's lid stands on the floor, at least 40 from the top and inside the width, in four views. How-to card: the level-7 step is bounded `to: 7` (it said "tilted" at 8), the level-8 step says one colour, and the notes are per level (1, 2–7, 8). IDs, windows and the dead `lap` row unchanged (slice 2 handles lap). `scripts/browser-check.mjs` `originals` shapes case: level 1 checks that a box tap, a floor wiggle and a far drop are free; level 8 drops a fitting but unasked shape first (one miss, it comes back), then the asked hole (glow).

### Feelings Faces slice 1: pools, the pet's own hug and scenery that answers (2026-10-20, [PR #673](https://github.com/rjdunlap/neo/pull/673))

Level 6 `EVENTS` 4 → 8 (rain → sad, butterfly → surprised landing clear of the face, lullaby → sleepy with falling bell notes and no night overlay, ball → happy); a round draws four different happenings. Level 8 `CARE_SCENARIOS` 2 → 5: balloon → hug, moon → pillow, plus `CARE_ONLY` happenings that never appear at level 6 (ice cream off a cone → sad → hug, snow → cold → scarf, empty picnic basket → hungry → apple). Deviation from the dive: it said 4 scenarios with a test of four distinct helpers, which is impossible because balloon and ice cream are both hug; the dive's optional picnic basket was added, so the five cover every helper and a round shows four different happenings and at least three helpers. `FRIENDS` 6 → 8 (pip, duck) for level 7 and the sticker. Voice: `feel.hug` is the pet's own preference ("{pet} loves a hug!" / "A big hug! {pet} likes that."), `feel.need-sad`, `feel.helped` and second variants for `feel.that`/`feel.how`, seven `feel.event-*` lines. A tap on a happening prop wobbles it (rotation driven from update, so an event's tween survives) and replays its sound, never a miss; props draw in a `front` layer above the big pet; clearProps kills a prop's tweens before destroying it. How-to: friends step scoped to level 7, new "Tap the pet to hear the question again." step at 2–6 and 8, finish per level (lap level 1 "Free play ends by itself after a few taps, with a sticker.", from 2 "Four questions, then a sticker."). `scripts/browser-check.mjs` `feelings8` takes `FEELINGS_LEVEL=6|8`.

## Pile Match levels 1–8 (2026-10-20)

[PR #680](https://github.com/rjdunlap/neo/pull/680), `claude/pile-match`, built from its [definition](../definitions/pile-match.md) on every default. New game `pile-match` in Counting Cove (now 11 games); preschool 1–4, pre-K 3–6, school 5–8, no toddler. Tap a free piece of a layered heap into a tray; three of a kind pop into the basket until the grass is clear. A 3-slot basket asking for one kind at levels 1–2 (a wrong kind is a miss), a free 5-slot tray from level 3; a full tray costs one miss per episode from level 4; covered touches and put-backs never count, and the two-miss glow plus a wander hint after three put-backs apply at 4–8. Heaps are frozen shapes (`shapes.ts`) with kinds dealt along a clearing route (`logic.ts`); the hint is a memoized search capped at 200,000 states, then the route fallback. The pet counts the pops by threes at levels 6–8. Files: `src/games/pile-match/` (`index`, `logic`, `shapes`, `logic.test`), registry, lands, voice lines `pile.*` and `game.pile-match`, how-to card, `pilematch` browser suite.

Deviations from the definition: logical views are always at least 1024×768, so its portrait usable width of 578 was wrong; the heap always draws at scale 1 and the layout test asserts it at four views. Level 1 always asks apples then shells, but its shape and arrangement are seeded from six flat shapes (no play count exists to author only the first round). The route fallback puts back only tray pieces not of the route's next kind, then takes in route order, which avoids a put-back/take loop. When a pop happens with a full tray, the third piece lands one slot past the tray for the moment before the three fly to the basket.

## Puddle Gobble levels 1–8 (2026-10-21)

[PR #687](https://github.com/rjdunlap/neo/pull/687), `claude/puddle-gobble`, built from its [definition](../definitions/puddle-gobble.md) on every default. New game `puddle-gobble` in Bubble Beach (now 8 games), region `puzzle-peaks`; toddler 1–2, preschool 2–4, pre-K 3–6, school 5–8; no lap, no couch. Files: `src/games/puddle-gobble/{logic.ts,logic.test.ts,index.ts}`, the registry, the `bubble-beach` list in `lands.ts`, voice lines (`game.puddle-gobble`, `gobble.*`), the how-to card and the `puddlegobble` browser case. Whole-screen steering is reused from Duckling Parade. Fit rule `t ≤ s` with tiers 1–4 as dots; growth is count-based (every 2 soaks at levels 2–3, every 3 at level 4, none at 1 and 5); numbers add 1 per soak at level 6 and their value at level 7; level 8 lands exactly on the pail, refuses an overshoot and lets a strip token go for free.

Accounting beyond the definition: a miss counts only when **aimed** (the finger's destination is on the thing, no fitting thing is nearer the finger, nothing soaked since she last aimed), so a pass-over on the way only wobbles and a let-go step-aside the game makes is never her aim. Found and fixed in browser play: a let-go thing landing back under the puddle stayed disarmed (the hint looped), and a game-moved target and pass-over soaks were counted as aimed misses. A disarmed thing re-arms when the puddle moves 30 units away or changes size. Rule tests (20): fit and growth, refusals, authored rounds, 500-seed generation with no fallback, no dead end at levels 1–7 (including sampled reachable states), level 8 exact totals and a reachable dead end, the hint reaching the pail within 8 steps from every reachable state, the bot with zero refusals over 200 seeds per plan, and layout at four views.

Idea row moved here from the notebook's candidates (the Big Fish, Little Fish row in the overlaps table now says it is covered):

| **Puddle Gobble** (Hole.io, 119 million downloads in 2025 per [PocketGamer.biz](https://www.pocketgamer.biz/the-most-downloaded-mobile-games-of-2025)) | Steer a puddle that soaks up things smaller than it and grows; bigger things wait their turn. Size comparison and ordering. No enemies: a thing too big just says "too big yet" | **Defined, ready (now built):** [definition](../definitions/puddle-gobble.md). Tiers 1–4 with count-based growth, so levels 1–7 can never dead-end; too-big things never block or count; Duckling Parade's steering; numbers and sums at 6–8; no chaser or timer | M |

## Peg Garden: every touch answers, a fair colour level and a true card, slice 1 (2026-10-21)

Branch `claude/peg-garden-every-touch`. On the island the whole screen takes touches (sea beside the board maps to the nearest ledge point, `ledgeX`); couch play keeps the board-sized hit area. Every touch at levels 1–3 makes a rising ring of bubbles at the finger. A touch while a pearl falls never makes a second pearl at levels 2–3 (a soft low pop) and at level 1 queues the next pearl (at most one waiting, never past the six). Level 3 drops with no drift (`driftOf`) and its stall guard runs too (`guided`: after `STALL_AT` = 2 pearls in a row with no new orange bloom, or while the hint is on, the pearl is let go over the `dropAt(…, 0, early)` spot); a guided pearl is never a miss. The hint adds a pulsing yellow ring on the ledge at that spot (`hintX`) beside the arrow, and `hinting` clears after an orange bloom so a later struggle can earn another hint. The ghost finger reads zero drift at 3. Dropped pearls step at a fixed 1/240 s (the ball engine's substep) so play matches `simulate()` at any frame rate (frame jitter had dropped the bot's sure-shot hit rate to 82%). Pixi 8's EventSystem never listens for DOM `pointercancel`, so the game listens on `window` (capture), a cancelled aim fires nothing, and the listener is removed in `destroy()`. The how-to card: level 1 "A tap while a pearl is falling drops the next one", steps 1–3 "Tap anywhere to drop a pearl from above that spot", a level 3 ring line, and the finish per level (1 after six pearls, 2 every flower has bloomed, 3+ the requested flowers). Rule tests: the colour level finishes when the same spot (left edge, middle, beyond right) is tapped every time within 16 pearls; the hinted spot touches a waiting orange bud on 40 boards; the bot's sure shots at the colour level all hit. Closes dive gaps 1–6, 8 and 9. Checks are in [VERIFICATION](VERIFICATION-2026-10-21.md#2026-10-21--peg-garden-every-touch-answers-slice-1).

## Swing Pals levels 1–6 (2026-10-20)

[PR #689](https://github.com/rjdunlap/neo/pull/689), `claude/swing-pals`, built from its [definition](../definitions/swing-pals.md) on every default. New game `swing-pals` in Rainbow Meadow (now 10 games), region `tinker-lab`; lap 1, toddler 1–2, preschool 2–3, pre-K 3–4, school 4–6; music `STYLES.paint`; no couch. An analytic pendulum, no engine: beats 1.0, 1.5 and 2.0 s from the hook alone (weight and height are not arguments); levels 1–3 push (an immediate tap at 1, a waiting push at 2, two swings on short and long ropes at 3; the bell latches, no misses); levels 4–6 choose the hook that makes her friend swing with the pet's, matched by phase.

Deviations from the definition (all in its [Built](../definitions/swing-pals.md#built) section): the glow at levels 4–6 fires once two different hooks have swung apart on a line (as written it could never fire; an open call in WAITING-ON-DEVELOPER); the finish lines the friends up to hop and ring in turn; seeded level 2 friends start still; layout numbers (seats at `view.h − 150`, long-rope scale 416 for two swings, the short hook 70 units forward); mouse and frog are local critter specs; "Show a hint" at levels 1–3 gives the next two pushes. Rule tests (23): beats independent of weight and height, level 1 any four taps ring, pushes at most one per swing, bell latch, together by phase, authored first lines, 500-seed generation with no fallback, layout at four views.

## Lift It levels 1–6 (2026-10-20)

[PR #692](https://github.com/rjdunlap/neo/pull/692), `claude/lift-it`, built from its [definition](../definitions/lift-it.md) on every default. New game `lift-it` in Rainbow Meadow (now 11 games), region `tinker-lab`; preschool 1–2, pre-K 2–4, school 3–6; music `STYLES.paint`; no couch. Files: `src/games/lift-it/{logic.ts,logic.test.ts,index.ts}`, the registry, the `rainbow-meadow` list in `lands.ts`, voice lines, the how-to card and the `liftit` browser case. A rule model, no engine: dragging the rope's end in strokes (measured from pointer movement; a held crate never slides back) hauls a crate to a friend on the tree's ledge; wheels dragged from a tray onto the branch hook and/or the crate's hook give four rigs (`direct` push, `fixed`, `movable`, `both`) with the ideal rule paws = ceil(w / strands), knots = h × strands. Levels: 1 Pull! (prebuilt branch wheel), 2 Push or pull, 3 Too heavy, 4 Twice the rope (knot counter and free knots guess), 5 Who pulls? (the request names ground or ledge pullers), 6 By the numbers (paws and knots cards before the haul).

Accounting: levels 1–4 record no misses. At 5–6 the first tug per rig per parcel on a rig that cannot lift or ends at the wrong pullers is a miss (pressing the greyed "nobody up there" handle counts the same), and a wrong number card is a miss and the right one lights. After a parcel's second miss the best rig's hooks (or the right card) glow, one hint per parcel. "Show a hint" pulls a stroke with her at levels 1–2 (one hint per request, no drawn pet paw: the stroke just happens with a spoken line) and glows at 3–6. One sticker per round; no journal entry. The ghost-finger bot hangs the best rig's wheels, answers cards right and hauls in strokes.

Not built: the definition's replay button (from level 3, replaying the last haul with knots counted aloud) and the drawn pet paw for the level 1–2 hint. The mechanism reviewer is still not named, so speech stays on the ideal rule. Rule tests (18): the rule, working rigs, hauling, authored lines, 500-seed generation per level with no fallback, layout at four views and ledge heights 1–3. Checks: [VERIFICATION](../VERIFICATION.md) 2026-10-20 (Lift It).

Idea row moved here from the notebook's candidates:

| **Lift It** (existing candidate; machines/tools study) | Deliver a parcel using different machines; compare ideal force and travel for a fixed load/height. A fixed pulley changes pull direction; a movable load pulley with two supporting strands trades half the ideal force for twice the rope travel | **Defined, ready (now built):** [definition](../definitions/lift-it.md). Levels 1–6: haul by strokes, then hang wheels on the branch or the crate (direct, fixed, movable on two strands, both); paws as force, knots as travel, every working rig counts; pullers and numbers at school. Ramps/levers later with their own rule | M |

## Attic Finds levels 1–2 (2026-10-21)

[PR #693](https://github.com/rjdunlap/neo/pull/693), `claude/attic-finds`, built from its [definition](../definitions/attic-finds.md) (slice H2 of the [everyday-life study](../deep-dives/subjects/daily-life-long-ago.md#build-slices-in-order)) on every default. New game `attic-finds` in Story Grove (now 11 games), region `story-grove`; preschool 1, pre-K 1–2, school 2; music `STYLES.paint`; no couch. Files: `src/games/attic-finds/{index.ts,logic.ts,logic.test.ts}`, the registry, the `story-grove` list in `lands.ts`, voice lines, the how-to card and the `atticfinds` browser case. Two invented objects (bird box, fish churn) per round, no third rolling object, nothing real, no journal discovery. Tapping a part opens a magnifier with the pet's line (nearest-spot resolution, a tick on looked parts); Turn shows the back, where a rod links crank to mover; a tap on the crank (or its handle, which also opens the handle clue) turns it one full turn of four clicks. Level 1 *Try it*: two pictured cards (bob, roll); the first card tap or crank is a free prediction, then "Which one happened?"; a wrong pick boings and replays the effect. Level 2 *Show the clue*: claim card, slot and Check; chips show "?" until looked at (tapping one opens its part, turning the thing if needed), and a looked chip goes into the slot by a tap or a drag.

Accounting: level 1 records no misses; two wrong picks glow the right card (one hint). At level 2 an unsupported Check is a miss (the part flashes, the chip returns, the pet explains), two misses glow the rod chip and Turn (one hint), and Check on an empty slot is free. "Show a hint" cranks and names the effect at level 1 and glows at level 2, once per object. Start again rebuilds the same round and keeps its misses and hints. Both objects shelved ends the round with one sticker. After 6 s idle the crank wiggles once (an invitation, not a hint). The ghost-finger bot plays both levels; its first level-1 trial is deliberately the wrong picture.

Not built (deviations from the definition): the "rub along it" crank gesture (a tap suffices) and tap-then-slot-tap for a looked chip (one tap puts it in the slot). Rule tests (19): objects, evidence, trials, 500-seed rounds, accounting and layout at four views. Checks: [VERIFICATION](../VERIFICATION.md) 2026-10-21 (Attic Finds).

## Fossil Dig levels 1–2 (2026-10-21)

Branch `claude/fossil-dig`, built from its [definition](../definitions/fossil-dig.md) (slice D2 of the [deep-time study](../deep-dives/subjects/deep-time.md#build-slices-in-order), E5 of the [earth-materials study](../deep-dives/subjects/earth-materials.md#build-slices-in-order)) on every default. New game `fossil-dig` in Tinker Lab (now 12 games), region `tinker-lab`; pre-K 1–2 only; no couch. Files: `src/games/fossil-dig/{index.ts,logic.ts,logic.test.ts}`, the registry, the `tinker-lab` list in `lands.ts`, voice lines, the how-to card, the `fossildig` browser case, slot art and `sfx.brush()`. Brush dust off a stone in one of three dig squares until 70% of the print's cells show (at most twelve pats), tap the flag of the square (red circle, blue triangle, yellow square), then tap the pinned reference leaf and Check. Level 1 matches by outline (star lobed against ribbon); level 2 by edge (saw-star toothed against star smooth) with look-closer badges and a magnifier. Authored first round per level per session (level 1 star in the blue square, level 2 saw-star in the yellow square). A finished round slides the record card into the field notebook and gives one sticker; nothing is saved or filed.

Accounting: a wrong flag and a wrong Check are misses; brushing, early flag, leaf or Check taps (a wiggle and the spoken step), an empty Check, swapping or returning a leaf and the magnifier are free. Two misses on a step glow its answer (one hint per step); "Show a hint" counts at most once per step and during brushing only repeats the words. Start again stops the old continuations and keeps misses and hints. The edge line is spoken only at level 2. The ghost-finger bot plays both levels.

Deviations from the definition's pixels: squares 290x230 landscape and 236x200 portrait with the stone scaled (0.7 / 0.57) inside its square; flags centred above the squares; the record card horizontal in both orientations; the notebook left of the top-right shell button; no separate magnifier badge on the stone; the level-2 hint opens the print's edge only; brush reach is 55 screen units. Rule tests (24): cases, rounds over 500 seeds, dust reveal at most twelve pats, tiling, reach, flags, accounting, layout at four views, two levels and three sites. Checks: [VERIFICATION](../VERIFICATION.md) 2026-10-21 (Fossil Dig).

## Trackway Tales levels 1–3 (2026-10-21)

Branch `claude/trackway-tales` ([PR #699](https://github.com/rjdunlap/neo/pull/699)), built from its [definition](../definitions/trackway-tales.md) (slice D5 of the [deep-time study](../deep-dives/subjects/deep-time.md#build-slices-in-order)). New game `trackway-tales`, school 1–3 only, region `tinker-lab`, home land **Story Grove** (now 12 games), nothing saved or filed; no couch. Files: `src/games/trackway-tales/{index.ts,logic.ts,logic.test.ts}`, the registry, `src/content/lands.ts`, voice lines `trackway-tales.*` and `game.trackway-tales`, the how-to card, the `trackwaytales` case in `scripts/browser-check.mjs`. Movement cards with a round Play replay an invented gray mud walker on a clear practice strip above a dried-mud strip (ghost prints, soft thuds; drag the strip to scrub). Tapping a card puts it on the study board, and Check slides its prints onto the mud and lights columns green in order until the first mismatch flashes orange with a spoken kind (extra mark, missing mark, toe the other way). Level 1 walk/hop (4 columns, 2 cards); level 2 which way (6 columns, 3 cards, the magnifier opens on a mark, tapping a mud mark says which way the toes point); level 3 a puddle over columns 3–4 (8 columns, 3 cards plus Can't tell: L3-pause fits two walkers so Can't tell is right; L3-switch is walk-then-hop and Can't tell is a miss). Each level's first round per session is authored (L1-walk with hop then walk; L2-left with column 5 magnified; L3-pause).

Accounting: replays, scrubs, the magnifier, an empty-slot Check and (level 3) a fitting card while another fits are free; a non-fitting Check or a wrong Can't tell is a miss. The second miss lights the glow on the right card, rings the telling column on both strips, replays the answer slowly and counts one hint; "Show a hint" does the same once per round. The ghost-finger bot plays all three levels.

Implementation notes: marks are stored by screen half (a left foot is on the upper half walking right, the lower half walking left), so walk and walk-back differ only by the toes, as the definition's level-2 test requires; single-gait movements carry on to the strip's end (the table's "walk x4 / hop x2" are the four-column picture). Portrait is 1024x1365 logical, so one centred layout serves every view (cards 140x240, Play 104 round).

Deviation from the definition: the home land is Story Grove, not Bubble Beach (decision 2). With ten games Bubble Beach's scenery test (`src/app/scenes/beach.test.ts`, rock pools) fails at school landscape, and Tinker Lab at 13 fails `lab.test.ts`; Barnyard and Story Grove both pass, and Story Grove (Owl Walk, Photo Safari: noticing outdoors) fits better than Barnyard. Rule tests (36): stamping, fitting, mismatch kinds, the puddle, Can't tell right once and wrong once, rounds over 500 seeds, accounting, hints, layout in four views. Checks: [VERIFICATION](../VERIFICATION.md) 2026-10-21 (Trackway Tales).

## Rhythm Neighbors: support truth and a real pool, slice 1 (2026-10-21)

[PR #708](https://github.com/rjdunlap/neo/pull/708), `claude/rhythm-neighbors-support-truth` ([deep dive](../deep-dives/rhythm-neighbors.md#build-slices-in-order), gaps 1-7 fixed; gap 8 partly: `PLANS` is keyed by level ID and `levels` is a `BandLevels` table, with no `order` yet). `REPLIES` holds twelve distinct shapes: Sound Garden's eight `RHYTHMS` plus LL, SLL, LSL and SLS (the dive listed SLS as an addition to the pool, but it was already in `RHYTHMS`; SLS is the old pool's shape, so SLS is the twelfth). Replies are drawn by length rather than cut: levels 3-4 give 2, 3, 3 notes (one 1-gap shape, then two distinct 2-gap shapes), levels 5-6 give 3, 3, 4. Calls (`CALLS`) are two or three knocks and never the reply's pattern; at 3-4 a call's knock count always differs from the reply's note count. Two-frog replies alternate from a seeded first frog. The teal frog croaks (new `sfx.ribbit(step = -4)`, two short sawtooth bursts gliding down, replacing `sfx.bell(4)`). Every round ends in a joinable song (`finaleEvents`, 4-5 s): the bird knocks its call, every frog on the pond plays the last reply together, her frog taps sound along un-judged (`finale` flag), then `ctx.finish`; level 6's "join a final duet" is now true, with the new voice line `neighbors.duet` replacing `neighbors.done`. Free levels 1-2 end by themselves after eight frog taps (`FREE_TAPS`), the arrow staying for an earlier stop; after six quiet seconds (`IDLE_WIGGLE`) a frog wiggles and croaks once per quiet spell, never counted. At levels 5-6 a gap over 2.5 s (`PAUSE_SECONDS`, `judgeEcho`'s threshold) inside an incomplete reply clears her taps with the line `neighbors.pause` and no miss (decision 2's lean). Extra taps already sounded (the dive said they were silent), but the cap recorded one extra tap, which turned it into a length miss; the cap is now the reply's length, so an extra tap sounds, is not recorded and costs nothing. The how-to finish and note are level-scoped.

Checks are in [VERIFICATION](VERIFICATION-2026-10-21.md#2026-10-21--rhythm-neighbors-support-truth-and-a-real-pool-slice-1).

## Roundup level 8 Make it fair (2026-10-21)

[PR #709](https://github.com/rjdunlap/neo/pull/709): slice 6 adds `mode: 'fair'` (PLANS[7]) for pre-K 3–8: an even herd of 6 or 8 of one kind, two pens of that kind and a single board with the animal and no number. `fairBell` accepts any equal non-zero split; a wrong bell is one miss (empty pens, one empty pen with nobody hopping out, or named counts with the extras leaving the fuller pen), two wrong bells give one hint (glow on the emptier pen's gate and the loose animals), and the finish says each pen's count is fair and that any loose animals are fine (decision 3's default). New `sfx.seesaw(level)` plays on every bell. The thirty-second trot-home goes into the emptier pen only while pens are uneven; once level, idle help wiggles only the bell (a review catch, now guarded and browser-checked). The bot (`fairNeeds`) fills the emptier pen to half each, then rings. Other levels and decisions unchanged.

Checks are in [VERIFICATION](VERIFICATION-2026-10-21.md#2026-10-21--roundup-level-8-make-it-fair-slice-6).

## Chain Reaction level 9 Two marbles (2026-10-21)

[PR #715](https://github.com/rjdunlap/neo/pull/715), `claude/chain-reaction-two-marbles`: a red and a blue marble share one 5x4 board (one fixed ramp, two loose ramps, three decoy sockets, one to three accepted designs, two machines per round). Each marble's way down needs its own loose ramp and the two ways never share a cell, so a single ramp cannot serve both; instead the ramp pool is shared and at least one spare socket lies on a marble's way, so a ramp put there for one marble knocks the other off course. Some placement rings one bell while the other misses (both directions), enforced by generation and tested. Cups show a red or blue dot, bells are tinted; red runs, then blue, and `works()` (used by run, hint, bot and solution counting) needs both. A failed run is a free experiment with a red and a blue trail, and tapping either cup replays both; the bulb counts a hint and glows a ramp that matters to the marble that missed. School becomes `{ min 3, max 9, order [3, 4, 5, 6, 9] }`; pre-K is unchanged (the dive's "pre-K 5" is ambiguous when its ladder ends at 3, left as an open choice). Voice `chain.two-start`, `-red`, `-blue`, `-both`, `-rang`; how-to level-9 steps. Known existing quirk: a ramp in the bottom row partly covers the bell beneath it. Checks are in [VERIFICATION](VERIFICATION-2026-10-21.md#2026-10-21--chain-reaction-level-9-two-marbles).

## Pet Kitchen: support truth, slice 1 (2026-10-21)

[PR #716](https://github.com/rjdunlap/neo/pull/716), `claude/pet-kitchen-support-truth` ([deep dive](../deep-dives/pet-kitchen.md#build-slices-in-order), the how-to, first-miss and silent-touch gaps, and the counted serve at 7; IDs, windows and the bot unchanged). `serveTrouble(plates, pieces)` in `logic.ts` names why a share or fraction serve missed: `uncut` (serve before the cut, the instruction repeats; a second uncut miss glows the cut that works), `short` (fewer pieces than friends, such as halves for four at level 2, checked before empty plates; new line `kitchen.short`, and on the second miss only the glow and `kitchen.recut` speak), `empty` (new line `kitchen.empty`) and `unequal` (the existing `kitchen.fair` and `kitchen.fraction-hint`); miss and hint accounting is unchanged. A touch on the uncut food wiggles it and repeats the instruction at most every 3 s, free and never a miss; six seconds of quiet before the cut bounce the cut buttons, and any touch on the counter resets the quiet. Level 7's fair serve counts the pieces aloud while each hops (new line `kitchen.fraction-count`, "{count}. That is {amount}! Thank you, chef!", the count from `countAloud(cut, served)`, such as "one quarter, two quarters, three quarters") and the round finishes after the line. The how-to closes its open ranges (halve step 8-8, pizza step 9-9, notes 7-7 and 8-8, a new 9-9 note that the round arrow puts the pizza back), says at levels 1-4 that touching the food repeats the instruction, and its misses note names a short serve and a friend with less.

Checks are in [VERIFICATION](VERIFICATION-2026-10-21.md#2026-10-21--pet-kitchen-support-truth-slice-1).

## Fluffy Salon: support truth, slice 1 (2026-10-21)

[PR #717](https://github.com/rjdunlap/neo/pull/717), `claude/fluffy-salon-support-truth` ([deep dive](../deep-dives/fluffy-salon.md#build-slices-in-order), gaps 1-4; IDs, levels and windows unchanged). Level 3's (`ask`) quiet-time hint now counts time without progress: `sinceProgress` resets only when a stroke touches strands with the tool that helps (`helps(look, strands, tool)` in `logic.ts`, true when the tool equals `toolFor(needs(...))`, computed before the touch) or when the helpful tool is picked; wrong-tool strokes, off-fur pats and wrong picks do not, so a child stuck on the wrong tool still gets the glow (`QUIET_HINT = 15`). A touch inside the touch rectangle that misses the fur makes the pet giggle and wobble (`poke(true)`, `sfx.giggle()`, 0.4 s cooldown), never counted. In free play, when the mirror appears the pet says the new line `salon.mirror` ("Look in the mirror when you are done!"), which becomes the repeated instruction, and the mirror wiggles every four seconds. A bug the audit missed: the mirror also appeared at level 3, which has no mirror, after 40 units of work, and a tap on it counted a miss; the reveal is now gated to play and tools. At a mirror check (levels 4-5) where the missing thing is the colour and at least half the strands have it, the pet says the new line `salon.more-color` ("Almost! A little more {fix}, where it glows.") and the strands still in the old colour glow with a pulsing warm-yellow halo (`moreLine`, `oldStrands`); the same glow comes with a colour hint. The 80 % rule is unchanged. The how-to is true: the finish at 1-2 is the mirror the pet asks for, at 3 each request finishes by itself, at 4-5 the mirror checks, with one note per level range. The browser check's `style`, `zigzag` and `cutShort` helpers now stop when the request moves on (they had kept styling the next request with the previous tool, a latent cause of spurious level-3 hints); level 3's scripted play fell from about 80 s to 22 s. Gaps 5-7 are untouched (later slices).

Checks are in [VERIFICATION](VERIFICATION-2026-10-21.md#2026-10-21--fluffy-salon-support-truth-slice-1).

## Little Conductor level 3, Two pictured requests, music M3 (2026-10-21)

[PR #718](https://github.com/rjdunlap/neo/pull/718), `claude/little-conductor-m3`, slice M3 of the [study](../deep-dives/subjects/music-instruments.md#build-slices-in-order), from the [definition](../definitions/little-conductor.md). Bands are now toddler 1–2, preschool 1–3, pre-K 2–3; skills gain "following a picture". A fourth seeded friend, the listener (never the bear or pip, never one of the band), holds a 200-unit sign showing the turtle or rabbit (the pads' picture and word) for the current half of the tune; requests are seeded, always one of each, both orders occur, and the band holds silent between phrases. A pad tap is her pick and auditions that half once at that pace (free, repeatable, never counted); Listen again replays the half at the picked pace; the green check in Done's place submits. Check with nothing picked wiggles the pads, says "Pick a pad first" and counts nothing. A wrong pick boings, bounces the sign, the pet names the requested pace (`wrong-turtle`/`wrong-rabbit`), one miss, the pick stays. Two wrong Checks on one half glow the requested pad and the band previews that pace once, counted as one hint, at most once per half; the pause sheet's Show a hint (`askForHint`, level 3 only) does the same and counts at most once per half. A right pick plays the half at that pace while the listener nods, then the sign turns to the second request; after the second half the band and listener bow and `ctx.finish({ misses, hints })` ends the round. Landscape keeps a 250-unit right column for the sign and listener; portrait puts the sign below the bandstand, right of the pads (`conductorLayout` tests cover levels 1–3 in four views). The co-play hint gains "be the listener and say what the sign shows"; the how-to card gained a level-3 step, finish and note; voice lines `instruct-sign`, `request-turtle/rabbit`, `wrong-turtle/rabbit`, `pick-first`, `match`, `hint-turtle/rabbit`.

Build-time decisions: the idle turtle-pad pulse is off at level 3 (it would point at an answer the sign may not ask for); `restart()` (Start again) is not implemented because `pauseLabels` shows no restart label on a child band, so it is not surfaced. The bot (`signBot`) reads the sign, taps that pad, waits for the audition and taps Check. The `conductor` browser suite defaults to levels 1,2,3 with a `CONDUCTOR_LEVELS` filter; level 3 runs in portrait (768×1024).

Checks are in [VERIFICATION](VERIFICATION-2026-10-21.md#2026-10-21--little-conductor-level-3-two-pictured-requests-music-m3).

## Sound Garden slice 2, the whole cast sings (2026-10-21)

[PR #725](https://github.com/rjdunlap/neo/pull/725), `claude/sound-garden-whole-cast`, from the [deep dive](../deep-dives/sound-garden.md) slice 2. `SINGERS` is six (bird, frog, bunny, turtle, bell, woodpecker); `makeCast(rng)` picks a seeded five in a seeded standing order (`CAST_SIZE` 5) for each level-1 round, and `singerToTap(cast, played)` cycles it for the ghost finger. `songFor(singer, tap)` gives each its role: bird `sfx.chirp` on steps 10-13, frog `sfx.ribbit` on pitched steps -3/-4 (replacing the unpitched croak), bunny four quick beats at 0.25 s and turtle two slow heavier tocks at 0.5 s over the same one-second span (`TEMPO_SPAN`, `FAST_BEATS`, `SLOW_BEATS`, so level 3's fast/slow cue is heard in free play), bell steps 5, 7, 9, 7, 10, woodpecker knock; the first beat sounds on touch-down and the singer bounces once per beat. How-to: the level 1 step names the roles and the five-of-six garden, and `finish` is level-scoped (level 1 ends after twelve songs; 2-4 and 6 questions answered; 5 rhythms echoed), since the old single line was untrue at levels 1 and 5. Rule tests: every cast singer heard within 12 taps (100 seeds), five distinct singers deterministic per seed with all six appearing across seeds and more than 20 orders, bird at least five steps above frog on integer steps, bunny and turtle gaps equal the fast and slow level's and filling the same span. Checks: [VERIFICATION](VERIFICATION-2026-10-21.md#2026-10-21--sound-garden-the-whole-cast-sings-slice-2).

Plan 9: one circle hole, three pieces in their own colour, a coded rim, `everyTouch` (a box tap gulps and giggles, a floor tap wiggles the nearest piece and names it, a far drop plops home) and `tapIn`: a touch let go within 0.4 s (`TAP_HOLD`) and moved under 120 units (`TAP_REACH`) from its floor spot hops the piece up and over into its hole; dragging to the hole still works. Three taps finish from any seed; misses and hints stay 0. `bands` gains `lap` `{ min 9, max 9, order [9] }`, toddler becomes `{ min 1, max 9, order [9, 1, 2, 3, 4, 5] }`, `entryLevels: [9]`; `describeLevel(9)` is "tap a shape and it hops in". Places is RW13's picker. Favorites lists hearted games newest first at any age, plays at `playBand` and speaks `explore.other-age` outside her band without re-pinning; empty shows one All games card and `explore.no-favorites`. Family play is one controller card to `go.couch(false, { explore: 'family', page: 0 })`; leaving returns to it. Pure logic is in `src/content/explore.ts` (`allGames`, `hasOthers`, `favoriteGames`, `ageOf`, `pageOf`, `pageCount`, `clampPage`, `exploreMemory`/`forgetExplore` from `playerChanged`); layout is `exploreGridLayout` and `PAGE_ARROW_RADIUS` in `src/creatures/home-layout.ts`; art is `tilesIcon`, `padIcon` and `src/ui/origin-art.ts`. `Origin` gains `{ explore, page, others? }` and `go.explore(entry?, page?, others?)`; classic's `explore` falls back to the map. A location's corner button at the root is a Back arrow to Places. The root's result screen (non-story, land or explore origin) turns the blue home into Back to [origin] (`result.back`) beside a white Home house (`after.house`, 100 units, 140 apart); the pause sheet's Leave uses the same origin. New voice lines: `explore.*` (pick, where, places, favorites, all, family, other-age, no-favorites, family-pads, page, others, mine) and `result.back`.

Open: couch's leave button still reads "Back to the island" for an Explore origin (couch code untouched). Checks are in [VERIFICATION](VERIFICATION-2026-10-21.md#2026-10-21--rw08-slice-1-explores-four-entries).

## Mail Carrier: seeded street, seven neighbours, the cart and a mirrored map, slice 2 (2026-10-21)

[PR #750](https://github.com/rjdunlap/neo/pull/750), `claude/mail-carrier-street` (code `8b4d5383` on base `a1a63aeb`). Street levels 1-5: the backdrop seed now comes from `ctx.rng` (drawn after the letters); each house gets a peaked or round roof, an optional chimney and window box (`houseLooks`); all mailboxes in a round share one colour from `MAILBOX_COLORS` (`mailboxColor`), never white, never a door colour in the round, and away from a near neighbour (`NEAR`: teal/blue, pink/red) when the round allows (it was always blue). One pool of seven neighbours (`NEIGHBORS`, the duck joins the street); the woods keeps six (`WOODS_NEIGHBORS`, without the dog, the bear's look-alike). The pet's cart: the companion (`ctx.companion()`, not `ctx.petSpec`) rides a small red cart on the road, kept clear of the guide corner (a decoration, `eventMode` none); on a post the letter drops into the cart, the cart rolls to the mailbox and the letter hops in, and a wrong mailbox hands it back to the cart and up to the envelope. Mirrored map (decision 5 default): each woods round (levels 6-8) draws `mirrored` with chance 0.5 after the woods and trips (`mapNode(n, mirrored)`, `mapX`, public `WoodsMail.mirrored`); the graph, stones and walks are unchanged. No level IDs, ranges, voice lines or how-to change. Rule tests (17) cover the pool and no dog-plus-bear over 300 seeds, the mailbox colour for every street plan over 300 seeds, house looks varying, mirrored nodes inside [0, 1] with equal edge lengths, and the reversed left-right order of the houses. The `woods` suite gained `MAIL_MIRRORED=1|0`. Checks are in [VERIFICATION](VERIFICATION-2026-10-21.md#2026-10-21--mail-carrier-seeded-street-the-cart-and-a-mirrored-map-slice-2).

### Bumper Garden level 8, Not this color (2026-10-22)

[PR #757](https://github.com/rjdunlap/neo/pull/757), `claude/bumper-not-this-color`, slice 5 of the [Bumper Garden dive](../deep-dives/bumper-garden.md): a `not: true` flag on a `color` plan (`PLANS[8]`: 8 flowers, 2 of the asked color, 3 each of two others) and `wantsColor(plan, color)` in `logic.ts`. The pet says "Bloom every flower that is not {color}! Shh, the {color} ones are sleeping."; 1.4 s later the asked-color flowers fall asleep (faded, tilted, a bobbing "z", on all three tables), are never wanted, and only chime when bumped. Accounting as level 3: a launch that blooms nothing wanted is a miss, two in a row glow the wanted flowers and aim the next launch (a counted hint; decision 5, hint accounting on "not" levels, is built on that default and still open). Pre-K order `[3, 4, 6, 8, 5, 9]`; ID 7 stays reserved; the finale wakes the sleepy flowers. Voice lines `bumper.not`, `bumper.again-not`; how-to level-8 step and finish lines, ring note widened to 8-9; couch catalog unchanged (levels 3-4).

## Ribbon Workshop levels 1-3 (fractions R2, 2026-10-22)

Branch `claude/ribbon-workshop` (code `291ed66b` on base `02671f92`), batch `ribbon-workshop 1` of the [definition](../definitions/ribbon-workshop.md), slice R2 of the [fractions study](../deep-dives/subjects/fractions.md#build-slices-in-order). New game `ribbon-workshop` (school only, range 1-3, region `counting-cove`), the twelfth game in Counting Cove (the lands layout test passed, so the Rainbow Meadow fallback was not used). `logic.ts` holds the model in quarter-ticks (four to the whole): a gift case is `{ target, tray }`, `place`/`return`/`snip`/`undo`, a six-tick bench capacity (an overfull place is refused, not a miss), equality by sum so every stock-feasible composition is accepted, and a breadth-first solver that gives `hintAction` (always a return from an overfull bench). A round is two gifts picked by the seeded RNG; at levels 2-3 the snip gift is always the second. Check on an empty bench is free; a non-empty wrong check is one miss with the gap or overhang flashed; the second miss glows the solver's next step (one hint); pause "Show a hint" (`hintLabel`) counts one unless the glow is up. The ghost finger follows the hint path and the demonstration plays one gift (the snip gift at 2-3). Voice lines `ribbon.*`, how-to card, `lands.ts` entry and `BROWSER_SUITE=ribbon` in `scripts/browser-check.mjs`. 11 rule tests (solvable, accepted compositions equal brute force, snip required on gift 2 at levels 2-3, undo exact, order independence, hint lowers distance by one or returns when overfull, capacity refusal, seeded rounds 0-199, accounting, voice lines, tray layout of at most two rows clear of the pet and buttons). Written fractions and the scissors-as-a-mode used the definition's defaults. A pre-K entry still waits on watching a child play level 1.

## Inchworm level 7, Big worms, little worms (measurement M2, 2026-10-22)

[PR #776](https://github.com/rjdunlap/neo/pull/776), `claude/inchworm-big-worms` (code `6d7d2a63` on base `e3a7002b`), batch `inchworm 1` of the [definition](../definitions/big-worms-little-worms.md), slice M2 of the [measurement study](../deep-dives/subjects/measurement-units.md#build-slices-in-order). One appended level of `inchworm`: the same stick measured with big worms, then little worms, then a number pad for the count the little (or big) worms made. Ranges: pre-K `{ 1..7, order [1, 2, 7] }` (the new top), school `{ 1..7, order [1, 2, 7, 3, 4, 5, 6] }`. Levels 1-6 generation is pinned by a test to its pre-change seed-7 output (15 logic tests). Deviation from the definition: the how-to card's demonstration (`ctx.demo`) measures one stick at level 7, since up to nine drags a stick would overrun the 75 s finger-demo limit (Jar Sort precedent), so it shows only a "little" question. Speech: "Now measure with little worms" waits for the last count to finish, because speech cancels the previous line. The `woods` suite's `worm` case honours `FROM_LEVEL`/`TO_LEVEL` and by default runs levels 1-7 (level 7 in portrait: one overhang drop on the full big-worm row, one wrong pad, glow and see-through big worm, screenshot `inchworm-7-hint`); its `lay()` helper now converts logical to screen coordinates. The default `fingerdemo` now picks level 7 as Inchworm's last level, so level 6 is no longer in its default run.

## Recipe Workshop levels 1–3 (2026-10-22)

Branch `claude/recipe-workshop-1-3` (PR #806): cooking study slices C2–C4, a new game, `recipe-workshop`, toddler 1, preschool 1–2, pre-K 2–3, region `cozy-village` but home land Painted Canyon (now 7 games). Pots of pretend fruit mash and a white base ("yogurt") go into a bowl as separate layers; the spoon stirs them into one blend; a star or leaf topper sits on top and Serve hands the bowl over. Level 1 My bowl (any non-empty bowl serves; no misses or hints), level 2 Follow the picture (one fruit and the base in either order, mixed after the last, Undo, no topper), level 3 Bowls for two (the companion and a seeded critter friend, each wanting a star or leaf; tap a bowl to choose it; mixing folds a topper in). Taps throughout, with a drag alternative (a pot, topper or the spoon onto either bowl acts there and selects it; a missed drag floats home). Rules in `logic.ts` (`check`, `hintFor`, a bounded non-repeating undo stack); only a failed non-empty Serve is a miss, two on one bowl glow the next step and count one hint, and Show a hint counts at most one per bowl. Decisions taken at their defaults: base spoken "yogurt", no topper at level 2, tap to mix. Decision 1's fallback applied: adding it to Cozy Village broke `village.test.ts` (no hedgerow fits at pre-K landscape with 12 visible games), which also affects Ready for Adventure and Second Chance Shed.

## Sea Lift levels 1–3 (2026-10-22)

Branch `claude/sea-lift-1-3`: integers study slice 2 (IN1), a new game, `sea-lift`, school levels 1–3 in Bubble Beach (now 10 games), region `counting-cove`. A glass lift on a pier with stops −5..5 and the water line as zero; landings zig-zag either side of the shaft at an even pitch, to scale (`shaftLayout` in `logic.ts`, resolving a touch to the nearest landing). Level 1 *Up or down?* five dock requests, level 2 *Through the water* five rides that cross zero, level 3 *Deepest first* three pick-up cases with at least two friends below the water. Misses are one per distinct wrong stop; two glow the right stop (one hint per request), a third sends the lift there itself. The friend's request is a fixed card left of the shaft (an arrow and one dot per stop), not a bubble beside the moving car; at level 3 a friend tap counts as its landing and replay is the card or the car. The first round of each level is authored per session and the demonstration opens on it, shortened. Speech lines are `sealift.*` ("N below the water", never minus or negative), with `sealift.aboard` added. Nothing is saved or filed in the journal. Shared changes: `beachPlan` in `src/app/scenes/beach.ts` tries three rocks, then one, then none, so a crowded shore keeps its two rock pools while layouts with room draw as before; and the workbox per-file precache bound in `vite.config.ts` rose from 3 MiB to 4 MiB because the routes bundle reached about 3.15 MB. Checks are in [VERIFICATION](../VERIFICATION.md). Open: iPad speech of "below the water", whether a six-year-old reads the zig-zag landings as one line, whether the splash at zero is noticed, whether a grown-up takes the caller part ([DEVICE-CHECKS](../DEVICE-CHECKS.md)). Sea Lift 4–5 (IN2) is next to define.
